It is follow-up 19's name (`detector-range-confirm-hold`: the range-aware memory confirmation with a lock) with `-confirm` on the end, and follow-up 24 did the same to follow-up 13 (`range-hold`, then `range-hold-confirm`). The word before the `-confirm` is the hold, so the confirmation is the hold's: **the confirmed clear of follow-up 21** (`K` of the last `min(30, occ)` readings free, `K` = 0.8 of the window). This round is therefore **follow-up 24 with the mark of follow-up 14** (one reading within 80 m, 2-of-3 beyond) and **follow-up 17's whole detector with the clear confirmed**: it asks whether the two smear cures, the range gate on the hold and the confirmed clear, still do one job when the mark is also range-aware, and whether the range-aware mark's advantage (follow-up 17's Q1) survives them. If it meant the ghost or the neighbour test (follow-ups 18 and 23) or something else, say so before the build.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown, and two detectors this round needs are not in the registry (`rm23f80` and `rm23re30k`, below). They, a registry gate row each, their mutants, the set HELD30 and this round's spec are added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD30 is: the same order as every earlier round.

**The rule** (follow-up 17's detector with the clear confirmed). Each cell keeps a window of its last 32 readings and `occ`. A free cell **beyond `near` = 80 m** of the sonar at the ping is marked when at least 2 of its last 3 readings were occupied, one **within `near`** (inclusive) at the first occupied reading; a marked cell **within `near`** is cleared when at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` of its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings were free, one **beyond `near`** in the **gated** form after one free reading and in the **ungated** form by the same `K`-of-`L` rule at every range.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 24): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with dropouts of 30 pings), **`WD30L`** (follow-up 21's: the false rate 0.01 uniform, dropouts of 30 pings leaking at 0.15), **`W`** (the uniform false rate of 0.01, no dropouts) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`rm23`** (follow-up 14's: the range-aware mark, a clear of one), **`rm23re30`** (follow-up 17's designated: the range-aware mark and the gated evidence hold, a run), **`rm23f80`** (the range-aware mark and the confirmed clear at every range), **`rm23re30k`** (**the designated: the range-aware mark and the gated evidence hold with the confirmed clear**) and **`m23re30k`** (follow-up 24's designated: the uniform 2-of-3 mark, the gated confirmed hold); under `WD30L` `inc`, `rm23re30`, `rm23f80` and `rm23re30k` (4); under `W` `inc` and `rm23re30k` (2); and `rm23re30k` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD30 = seeds 3001 to 3060 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm23re30k` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** Three prices meet. The range gate loses a cell that a dropout beginning far away takes (follow-up 17's tension, follow-up 18's J1); the confirmed clear clears a leaking dropout sooner (follow-up 21's A4); and the range-aware mark marks a near false return at the first reading (follow-up 9's K4). Only `WD30L` has the leak and only `R3D30` has the far dropouts the range law makes common, so a detector that holds both bars has paid all three.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason).**
* **V1** the confirmed clear pays in the range-aware detector: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30k` reaches at least 5 more than `rm23re30`. (Follow-up 21's A2 and follow-up 24's I2, asked of follow-up 17's detector.)
* **V2** and the range-aware mark's advantage survives it: under `R3D30`, `rm23re30k` reaches at least 5 more than `m23re30k`. (Follow-up 17's Q1 predicted ten.)
* **V3** the gate on the hold costs under the joint stress whatever the clear: under `R3D30`, `rm23f80` reaches at least 5 more than `rm23re30k`. (Follow-up 18's J1.)
* **V4** behind the gate the confirmed clear changes little where the dropouts leak: under `WD30L`, `rm23re30k` is within 8 of `rm23re30` (either side). (Follow-up 24's I2.)
* **V5** `rm23re30k` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **V6** and under `WD30L`.
* **V7** it is still a cure where the law does not hold: under `W`, `rm23re30k` reaches at least 10 more than `inc`. (Follow-up 17's Q6.)
* **V8** on a sea with no faults `rm23re30k` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`rm23f80` against `rm23re30` under `WD30L`, the per-kind and per-gap counts and the pooled occupied cells at the last plan are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 2-of-3, 1-of-1, a cap of 30 and `f` = 0.8 are the right numbers; that the range at the ping is the right gate (follow-up 15); that the pair of cures is better than any other treatment of the smear; that the leak is a measured flicker (it is made up, follow-up 21). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

## FOLLOW-UP 26: WHICH CONFIRMATION WHEN THE HOLD IS THERE, AND IT KNOWS HOW FAR (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm-hold-range", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 26 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 20's name (`detector-confirm-hold`: which `M`-of-`N` mark when the evidence hold is there) with `-range` on the end, the move follow-up 17 made on follow-up 16. Taken as **follow-up 20's question asked of the range-aware mark**: with the evidence hold of cap 30 in place at every range, which rule should the mark use **beyond 80 m**, with one reading within it? Follow-up 14 compared `rm23`, `rm24` and `rm34` with a clear of one; follow-up 20 compared the family with the hold but the same rule at every range; this round is the table those two leave: the range-aware family with the hold. If it meant the hold range-gated as well (follow-ups 17, 19 and 24's `re30` forms), say so before the build.

**The question.** Under the range law the far zone is where the false returns are (0.06 a cell a ping at 200 m against 0.0054 at 60 m) and where the vessel has time, so a strict far rule is cheap there; near the vessel a delay costs a collision. Follow-up 20 bet that strictness is affordable under a hold; this round bets it is **more** affordable far away, and puts the price (a near false return marked at the first reading, the law failing) beside it.

**The rule** (follow-up 20's detector with the near rule): a free cell **beyond `near` = 80 m** from the sonar at the ping is marked when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied, one **within `near`** (inclusive) at the first occupied reading; a marked cell is cleared after `min(30, occ)` free readings in a row, at every range (the evidence hold of follow-up 12 as in follow-up 20).

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 25): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings), **`WD30`** (follow-up 16's: the false rate **uniform** at 0.01 with the same dropouts: the law NOT holding) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the eight detectors **`m11e30`** (a mark of one everywhere: the hold alone), **`rm22e30`**, **`rm33e30`**, **`rm23e30`**, **`rm24e30`** and **`rm34e30`** (**the designated**: 3-of-4 beyond 80 m, one reading within), and, for the comparison with the same rule everywhere, **`m34e30`** (follow-up 20's designated) and **`m23e30`** (follow-up 16's); under `WD30` `m11e30`, `rm23e30`, `rm34e30` and `m34e30` (4); and `rm34e30` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD31 = seeds 3101 to 3160 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm34e30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells, the range law with dropouts. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`WD30` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar: they ask what the bet on the law costs.)

**The tension, said before the data.** A strict far rule delays marking a wall the long sonar sees early, and a dropout that begins before the confirmation hides the obstacle from the hold altogether (follow-up 16's tension), which the range law makes likeliest far away; and the near zone, marked at the first reading, takes a near false return into the plan at once.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AA because every single letter is taken).**
* **AA1** the family still collapses under a hold: under `R3D30`, the five confirming rules (`rm22e30`, `rm33e30`, `rm23e30`, `rm24e30`, `rm34e30`) have pooled counts within 20 of each other. (Follow-up 20's Y1, with the near zone alike in all five.)
* **AA2** every far confirmation repairs the range-law false returns: under `R3D30` each of the five reaches at least 15 more than `m11e30`. (Follow-up 9's K1.)
* **AA3** strictness pays far away where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `rm34e30` reaches more than `rm23e30`.
* **AA4** and a run of three is dearer than a run of two there: under `R3D30`, `rm33e30` reaches at least 5 fewer than `rm22e30`. (A far real obstacle at the sonar's edge is read three times in a row about a fifth of the time.)
* **AA5** `rm34e30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AA6** the bet on the law has a price: under `WD30`, `rm34e30` reaches at least 10 fewer than `m34e30`. (Follow-up 9's K4: the near zone marks a uniform false return at its first reading.)
* **AA7** range-awareness pays where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `rm34e30` reaches at least 5 more than `m34e30`. (No delay near.)
* **AA8** on a sea with no faults `rm34e30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m23e30` against `rm23e30` under `R3D30`, `rm34e30` against `m11e30` under `WD30`, the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m and 3-of-4 are the right numbers or that the far rule should be a step and not a smooth function of range; that the readings of a cell are independent; that the range law is true of a real sonar; that a gated hold (follow-up 24) or a confirmed clear (follow-up 21) changes the order of the rules. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs four detectors the registry does not have (`rm22e30`, `rm33e30`, `rm24e30`, `rm34e30`, each a registry row), the set HELD31 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD31 is (as follow-up 25's are).

## FOLLOW-UP 27: WHICH RANGE THE CONFIRMED HOLD GOES BY (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-range-confirm", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 27 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 15's name (`detector-hold-range`: which range the hold goes by, and whether the gate is a step or a ramp) with `-confirm` on the end, and follow-up 24's name (`detector-range-hold-confirm`) with the emphasis moved the way follow-up 15 moved follow-up 13's. The confirmation is the hold's, as in follow-up 24: **the confirmed clear of follow-up 21**. Taken as **follow-up 15's question asked of the confirmed hold**: the clear is `K` = 0.8 of the last `min(30, occ)` readings free, the hold's length is gated by a range, and the range is the cell's range **at the ping**, **at its marking** or **the nearest it has read occupied from**, as a **step** at 80 m or a **ramp** to 200 m. The mark is follow-up 24's (2-of-3 at every range), so the hold is the only thing that varies. If it meant something else, say so before the build.

**The question.** Follow-up 15 asked which range suits a hold that is a run of free readings. A confirmed clear is not a run: it clears a cell that flickers occupied, and it clears a leaking dropout sooner. Whether the choice of range still matters when the clear tolerates an occupied reading in five is the question, and it is asked under the two conditions that carry the two prices (far dropouts, a leaking dropout).

**The rule** (follow-up 24's detector with the gate's range and shape chosen): a free cell is marked when at least 2 of its last 3 readings were occupied, at every range; a marked cell is cleared when at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` of its last `L` readings were free, where `L = min(g(r), occ)` and `g(r)` is **step**: 30 if `r` <= 80 m, else 1; or **ramp**: `max(1, round(30 (1 - r / 200 m)))`; `r` is the cell's range **`ping`** (now), **`mark`** (at its marking) or **`min`** (the least range it has read occupied from since it was last cleared).

**Conditions** (everything else as follows-ups 3 to 26): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with dropouts of 30 pings and the range law), **`WD30L`** (follow-up 21's: the false rate 0.01 uniform, dropouts of 30 pings leaking at 0.15) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the 2-of-3 mark): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the seven detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23e30k`** (the hold at every range), **`m23re30k`** (follow-up 24's designated: step, `ping`), **`m23mk30k`** (step, `mark`), **`m23mn30k`** (step, `min`), **`m23rp30k`** (ramp, `ping`) and **`m23rn30k`** (**the designated: ramp, `min`**, as follow-up 15's); under `WD30L` `inc`, `m23e30k`, `m23re30k` and `m23rn30k` (4); and `m23re30k` and `m23rn30k` on a sea with no faults (2). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD32 = seeds 3201 to 3260 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m23rn30k` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** The ramp is shorter than the step inside 80 m and longer outside it, so it gives up some near hold for the 80 to 160 m band, where the fix smear is dearest; the confirmed clear already cleans that smear (follow-up 21), so the ramp may buy nothing the clear does not; and `min` keeps a wall held after the vessel has passed it, which costs only if the plan uses the cells behind.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AB).**
* **AB1** the range at marking is the wrong range: under `R3D30`, `m23mk30k` reaches at least 10 fewer than `m23re30k`. (Follow-up 15's T1: a cell marked from 150 m is a far cell for ever.)
* **AB2** the nearest range ever read does as well as the range now: under `R3D30`, `m23mn30k` is within 10 of `m23re30k` (either side). (Follow-up 15's T2.)
* **AB3** the ramp does as well as the step: under `R3D30`, `m23rp30k` is within 10 of `m23re30k` (either side). (Follow-up 15's T3.)
* **AB4** the designated covers the long dropout: under `R3D30`, `m23rn30k` reaches at least 10 more than `inc`.
* **AB5** `m23rn30k` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AB6** and under `WD30L`.
* **AB7** the gate costs against a hold at every range under the joint stress: under `R3D30`, `m23e30k` reaches at least 5 more than `m23rn30k`. (Follow-up 18's J1 and follow-up 24's I1.)
* **AB8** on a sea with no faults `m23rn30k` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m23re30k` against `m23rn30k` under `WD30L`, the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 200 m, 30, 0.8 and 2-of-3 are the right numbers; that a step and a linear ramp are the only shapes or that the minimum since the last clear is the right memory (follow-up 15's caveats stand); that the leak is a measured flicker. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs four detectors the registry does not have (`m23mk30k`, `m23mn30k`, `m23rp30k`, `m23rn30k`, each a registry row), the set HELD32 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD32 is (as follow-ups 25 and 26's are).

## FOLLOW-UP 28: THE NEAR ZONE CONFIRMED TOO, GRADED BY RANGE (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-confirm-range-confirm", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 28 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 17's name (`detector-hold-confirm-range`: the whole detector, every end knowing how far) with `-confirm` on the end. The trailing confirmation is **not** taken as the hold's confirmed clear: that is follow-up 25 (`range-confirm-hold-confirm`), whose words say it. It is taken as **a second confirmation, in the other zone**: every range-aware mark in the notes (follow-ups 9, 10, 14, 17, 18, 19, 26) marks within 80 m at the **first** reading, and the price of that bet is registered each time (follow-up 9's K4, 14's S5, 17's Q5, 26's AA6: the near zone takes a uniform false return into the plan at once). This round **confirms the near zone as well**, with a rule lighter than the far one, so the confirmation is graded by range: strict far, light near. If it meant the hold's confirmed clear, that is follow-up 25; if it meant something else, say so before the build.

**The question.** A false return marked within 80 m is the dearest kind (the vessel meets it), and it is the kind the range-aware mark lets through when the false rate does not fall near the vessel (follow-up 7's `W`, a uniform 0.01). A near rule of two readings of three costs a ping or two of delay on a real wall and cuts a uniform false return to its square. Does the near confirmation repair the uniform sea without costing the range-law sea?

**The rule** (follow-up 26's detector with a near rule other than one reading): a free cell **beyond `near` = 80 m** of the sonar at the ping is marked when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied, one **within `near`** (inclusive) when at least `Mn` of its last `Nn` were; a marked cell is cleared after `min(30, occ)` free readings in a row at every range (the evidence hold of follow-up 12, as in follow-up 26).

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 27): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law) and **`WD30`** (follow-up 16's: the false rate **uniform** at 0.01 with the same dropouts): the sea where the law holds and the sea where it does not; and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under each of `R3D30` and `WD30` the six detectors **`m11e30`** (a mark of one everywhere: the hold alone), **`m23e30`** (2-of-3 everywhere: follow-up 16's), **`m34e30`** (3-of-4 everywhere: follow-up 20's), **`rm34e30`** (3-of-4 beyond 80 m, one reading within: follow-up 26's designated), **`n22f34e30`** (3-of-4 beyond, 2 in a row within) and **`n23f34e30`** (**the designated: 3-of-4 beyond 80 m, 2-of-3 within**) (12 arms); and `n23f34e30` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD33 = seeds 3301 to 3360 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `n23f34e30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30` and under `WD30`**: six cells, both seas. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** The near zone is where a delay costs a collision, and a dropout that begins before the near confirmation hides the obstacle from the hold altogether (follow-up 16's tension); the light rule is bought against the uniform sea and paid for in the range-law sea, where near false returns are rare and the delay buys little.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AC).**
* **AC1** a near confirmation repairs the uniform sea the range-aware mark could not: under `WD30`, `n23f34e30` reaches at least 10 more than `rm34e30`. (Follow-up 26's AA6 is the price it removes.)
* **AC2** and costs little where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `n23f34e30` is within 8 of `rm34e30` (either side).
* **AC3** graded beats uniform strictness where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `n23f34e30` reaches at least 5 more than `m34e30`. (No strictness near.)
* **AC4** and beats a uniform light rule: under `R3D30`, `n23f34e30` reaches at least 5 more than `m23e30`. (A strict rule far, where the false returns are.)
* **AC5** `n23f34e30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AC6** and under `WD30`.
* **AC7** the memory in the near rule is worth little: under `R3D30`, `n22f34e30` is within 8 of `n23f34e30` (either side). (The near zone is a sixth of the sector's area.)
* **AC8** on a sea with no faults `n23f34e30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m11e30` against `m34e30` under each sea, the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 3-of-4 far and 2-of-3 near are the right numbers or that the grading should be a step and not a smooth function of range; that the hold at every range and a run-clear are the right hold (follow-ups 13 to 25 are that table); that the range law is true of a real sonar. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs two detectors the registry does not have (`n22f34e30` and `n23f34e30`, each a registry row; `rm34e30` is follow-up 26's row), the set HELD33 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD33 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 29: THE SECOND HOLD, ON THE CONFIRMED HOLD (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-hold-confirm-hold", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 29 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 24's name (`detector-range-hold-confirm`: the range-gated hold with the clear confirmed) with `-hold` on the end, the move follow-up 18 made on follow-up 17. Follow-up 18's reading is kept: **a second hold, on what has been confirmed**, in the two ways it can be done: take the range gate off the hold (the confirmed evidence hold at every range) and add **the ghost** (a cell cleared after at least 4 occupied readings is re-marked at one reading for 60 readings of that cell; no neighbour test: that is follow-up 23's). The four combinations are flown. The tension it answers is registered in both predecessors: the gate lets a dropout that begins far away take the cell (follow-up 17, follow-up 24's I1), and the confirmed clear clears a leaking dropout sooner (follow-up 21's A4). If it meant only one of the two, or something else, say so before the build.

**The rule** (follow-up 24's detector with two switches). Each cell keeps a window of its last 32 readings and `occ`. A free cell is marked when at least 2 of its last 3 readings were occupied, at every range; a marked cell is cleared when at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` of its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings were free, where `L` is 30 for a marked cell **within 80 m** and 1 **beyond** it in the **gated** form, and 30 at every range in the **ungated** form; **with the ghost**, a cleared cell with `occ` of at least 4 keeps its window and evidence for 60 readings, and a reading of it as occupied in that time marks it at once with `occ` put back to 4.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 28): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's), **`WD30L`** (follow-up 21's: the false rate 0.01 uniform, dropouts of 30 pings leaking at 0.15), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix: where the ghost is tested, its false returns repeating by design) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the 2-of-3 mark): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under each of `R3D30` and `WD30L` the five detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23e30k`** (the confirmed hold at every range: follow-up 24's), **`m23re30k`** (the gated confirmed hold: follow-up 24's designated), **`m23re30kg`** (gated, with the ghost) and **`m23e30kg`** (**the designated: at every range, with the ghost**) (10 arms); under `P` `m23re30k` and `m23re30kg` (2); and `m23e30kg` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD34 = seeds 3401 to 3460 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m23e30kg` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** The hold at every range is the hold follow-up 13 bet against (it brings back the fix smear far from the vessel; the confirmed clear is the smear's cure, so this may be cheap here); and the ghost re-marks a persistent phantom at its first reading, which the persistent mix is built to find. The ghost is also the one stage that can undo the confirmed clear's own price: a leaking dropout cleared sooner is a ghost cell that the next occupied reading restores.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AD).**
* **AD1** the gate costs under the joint stress: under `R3D30`, `m23e30k` reaches at least 5 more than `m23re30k`. (Follow-up 18's J1 and follow-up 24's I1, asked of the confirmed hold.)
* **AD2** the ghost helps the gated hold: under `R3D30`, `m23re30kg` reaches at least 5 more than `m23re30k`. (Follow-up 18's J3.)
* **AD3** and little when the hold is everywhere: under `R3D30`, `m23e30kg` is within 8 of `m23e30k` (either side). (Follow-up 18's J4.)
* **AD4** the ghost answers the leak: under `WD30L`, `m23e30kg` reaches at least 5 more than `m23e30k`. (The confirmed clear clears a leaking dropout sooner; the ghost restores it at one reading.)
* **AD5** and the ghost has a price under the persistent mix: under `P`, `m23re30kg` reaches at least 5 fewer than `m23re30k`. (A persistent false return that passed the confirmation is re-marked at its first reading each time it is cleared.)
* **AD6** `m23e30kg` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AD7** and under `WD30L`.
* **AD8** on a sea with no faults `m23e30kg` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m23e30kg` against `m23re30k` under each sea, the per-kind and per-gap counts and the number of ghost re-marks (`detStats.ghostMarks`) are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers for the ghost, that the ghost should be a count of readings and not a length of time, that 80 m, 2-of-3, 30 and 0.8 are the right numbers; that the leak is a measured flicker. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs two detectors the registry does not have (`m23e30kg` and `m23re30kg`, each a registry row), the set HELD34 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD34 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 30: THE GHOST FOR THE STRICT FAR MARK (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm-hold-range-hold", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 30 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 26's name (`detector-confirm-hold-range`: which range-aware mark when the evidence hold is in place) with `-hold` on the end, the move follow-ups 18 and 29 made on 17 and 24. Their reading is kept: **a second hold, on what has been confirmed**, which for a detector whose hold already runs at every range is **the ghost** (a cell cleared after at least 4 occupied readings is re-marked at one reading for 60 readings of that cell; no neighbour test: that is follow-up 23's). The reason it belongs with follow-up 26's family: a **strict far rule is slow to re-mark**. An evidence hold clears a cell watched for ten readings after ten free ones, and a dropout of 30 pings does that to every cell that had not been watched for thirty; the cell then has to be confirmed again, and 3 of 4 is the slowest of the family to do it. The ghost is the hold on the confirmation. If it meant the hold range-gated (follow-ups 17, 19, 24) or something else, say so before the build.

**The rule** (follow-up 26's detector with a switch). A free cell **beyond `near` = 80 m** of the sonar at the ping is marked when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied, one **within `near`** (inclusive) at the first occupied reading; a marked cell is cleared after `min(30, occ)` free readings in a row at every range; **with the ghost**, a cell cleared with `occ` of at least 4 keeps its window and evidence for 60 readings and a reading of it as occupied in that time marks it at once with `occ` put back to 4.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 29): **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the white faults with the range law and the false rate 0.03 at its mean, with dropouts of 30 pings), **`WD30`** (follow-up 16's: the false rate uniform at 0.01 with the same dropouts: the law NOT holding), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`: where the ghost is tested, its false returns repeating by design) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`m11e30`** (the hold alone), **`rm23e30`** (2-of-3 far, one reading near: follow-up 18's), **`rm23e30g`** (the same with the ghost: follow-up 18's designated), **`rm34e30`** (3-of-4 far: follow-up 26's designated), **`rm34e30g`** (**the designated: 3-of-4 far, with the ghost**) and **`m34e30`** (3-of-4 everywhere: follow-up 20's) (6 arms); under `WD30` `m11e30`, `rm34e30`, `rm34e30g` and `m34e30` (4); under `P` `rm34e30` and `rm34e30g` (2); and `rm34e30g` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD35 = seeds 3501 to 3560 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rm34e30g` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`WD30`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The ghost re-marks a persistent phantom at its first reading (follow-up 18's tension, follow-up 29's AD5), and a strict far rule is the stage most likely to let a persistent phantom through the confirmation only rarely, so the price may be small here. And the ghost does nothing for the first marking, which is where a strict far rule is slowest; a dropout that begins before the confirmation hides the obstacle from the hold altogether.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AE).**
* **AE1** the ghost helps a strict far rule: under `R3D30`, `rm34e30g` reaches at least 5 more than `rm34e30`. (Re-marking after a dropout at one reading and not at 3 of 4.)
* **AE2** and helps it more than a light one: `rm34e30g` + `rm23e30` reaches at least 3 more than `rm34e30` + `rm23e30g` under `R3D30` (the ghost's gain to the strict rule exceeds its gain to the light rule by at least 3).
* **AE3** strict beats light with the ghost where the law holds: under `R3D30`, `rm34e30g` reaches more than `rm23e30g`.
* **AE4** `rm34e30g` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AE5** the ghost has a price under the persistent mix: under `P`, `rm34e30g` reaches at least 5 fewer than `rm34e30`.
* **AE6** the bet on the law has a price: under `WD30`, `rm34e30g` reaches at least 10 fewer than `m34e30`. (Follow-up 26's AA6.)
* **AE7** the pair still cures the false returns: under `R3D30`, `rm34e30g` reaches at least 15 more than `m11e30`. (Follow-up 26's AA2.)
* **AE8** on a sea with no faults `rm34e30g` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`rm34e30` against `m11e30` under `WD30`, the per-kind and per-gap counts and the number of ghost re-marks are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 3-of-4, 80 m, 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers; that a ghost with the neighbour test of follow-up 23 would not change the price under `P`; that the range law is true of a real sonar. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs one detector the registry does not have (`rm34e30g`; `rm34e30` is follow-up 26's row), the set HELD35 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD35 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 31: THE CONFIRMED CLEAR THAT KNOWS HOW FAR (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-hold-confirm-range", asked while the DEV runs of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ANY SET OF 8 TO 31 IS FLOWN, so nothing below was shaped by a result)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 24's name (`detector-range-hold-confirm`: the range-gated hold with the clear confirmed) with `-range` on the end, the move follow-up 17 made on follow-up 16. The range-aware **mark** (follow-up 14's) is not what is meant: `range-hold-confirm` with a range-aware mark is follow-up 25's detector, `rm23re30k`. It is taken as **the confirmation itself knowing how far**: follow-up 21's confirmed clear clears a marked cell when `K = ceil(f L)` of its last `L` readings were free, `f` = 0.8 at every range; this round lets **`f` depend on the cell's range at the ping**, one fraction within 80 m and another beyond. The hold is at every range (the gated hold makes `L` one beyond 80 m, and a window of one has no fraction). If it meant something else, say so before the build.

**The question, and why a range can matter to a fraction.** The confirmed clear has two registered effects pulling opposite ways: it cures the fix smear (a long hold thickens a wall; `f` < 1 clears the edge cells that flicker; follow-up 21's A1 and A2) and it clears a leaking dropout sooner (follow-up 21's A4). Where each matters is not the same place. A leaking dropout cleared **near** the vessel is the collision; a smeared wall **far** away is a plan that closes a gap the vessel will see better later. So the bet is a **strict fraction near** (a run: a flicker does not clear what the vessel is about to meet) and a **tolerant fraction far** (clean the smear where it is cheap); and the opposite arm is flown to see whether the bet is the right way round.

**The rule** (follow-up 21's detector with two fractions): a free cell is marked when at least 2 of its last 3 readings were occupied, at every range; a marked cell is cleared when at least `K = ceil(f x L)` of its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings were free, where `f` is `fNear` for a cell **within 80 m** of the sonar at the ping (inclusive) and `fFar` for one **beyond** it. `fNear` = `fFar` is follow-up 21's detector. (This needs one new field in the module, `clear.fNear`, a gate row and its mutants, as well as registry rows: see below.)

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 30): **`WD30L`** (follow-up 21's: the false rate 0.01 uniform, dropouts of 30 pings leaking at 0.15: where the leak is) and **`R3D30`** (follow-up 17's: the range law with dropouts of 30 pings and no leak: where the smear is) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix, the 2-of-3 mark and the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under each of `WD30L` and `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23`** (one free reading to clear), **`f100`** (a run at every range: follow-up 21's), **`f80`** (0.8 at every range: follow-up 21's designated), **`fn100f80`** (**the designated: a run within 80 m, 0.8 beyond**) and **`fn80f100`** (0.8 within, a run beyond: the opposite bet) (12 arms); and `fn100f80` on a sea with no faults. Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD36 = seeds 3601 to 3660 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `fn100f80` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30L` and under `R3D30`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise.

**The tension, said before the data.** Both effects of the confirmed clear are felt near the vessel, where the 24 m gaps are met, so a strict fraction there gives up the smear cure exactly where it paid (follow-up 21's A1 is about the gaps); the bet is that the leak's price near is the larger of the two.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AF).**
* **AF1** a strict fraction near answers the leak: under `WD30L`, `fn100f80` reaches at least 3 more than `f80`. (A run within 80 m is held by the leaking dropout's flicker where `f` = 0.8 lets it go.)
* **AF2** and gives up the smear cure near: under `R3D30`, `fn80f100` reaches at least 3 more than `fn100f80`. (Follow-up 21's A1: the cure pays in the gaps, and the gaps are met near.)
* **AF3** the confirmed clear still cures the smear everywhere: under `R3D30`, `f80` reaches at least 5 more than `f100`. (Follow-up 21's A2.)
* **AF4** and still pays for it under the leak: under `WD30L`, `f100` reaches at least 5 more than `f80`. (Follow-up 21's A4.)
* **AF5** `fn100f80` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AF6** and under `WD30L`.
* **AF7** the far fraction is worth little: under `R3D30`, `fn80f100` is within 8 of `f80` (either side).
* **AF8** on a sea with no faults `fn100f80` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m23` and `inc` against the rest, the per-kind and per-gap counts are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 80 m, 2-of-3, 30, 1.0 and 0.8 are the right numbers or that the fraction should be a step in range and not a smooth function; that the leak is a measured flicker (it is made up, follow-up 21); that the gated hold of follow-up 24 or the ramp of follow-up 15 would not change the order. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs **a new field in the module** (`clear.fNear`, default `f`: validation, the hold's choice by range, `describe`, gate rows and mutants), two registry rows (`fn100f80`, `fn80f100`), the set HELD36 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD36 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 32: THE HOLD THAT REMEMBERS WHAT IT HELD (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-range-hold", asked while the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, but NOT blind to results: see the paragraph after the next)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 15's name (`detector-hold-range`: which range the hold goes by, and its shape) with `-hold` on the end, the move follow-up 18 made on follow-up 17 and follow-up 29 on follow-up 24. Follow-up 15's detector is kept as it was (a mark of one occupied reading; the evidence hold of cap 30, gated by a ramp to 200 m on the nearest range the cell has been read occupied from, or on the range at the ping) and a **second hold is put on the evidence**: a cell that had earned a hold (at least 4 occupied readings since it was last cleared) and is cleared anyway, by a dropout longer than its hold (the ramp holds 12 readings at 120 m and 6 at 160 m, against a dropout of 30 pings), **comes back with a part of that evidence when it is next read occupied**, where follow-up 15's detector brings it back as a cell of one reading, which the next dropout clears at the first free reading. This is follow-up 18's *ghost* (a cleared cell keeps its window and evidence for 60 readings of that cell; read occupied in that time it is marked again, with its evidence put back to 4).

**What the module does with that, said now, from a read-only run of the frozen module (a scratch script, not committed): nothing.** The ghost as built is a no-op under a mark of one. In `apply` the mark rule's branch comes first and marks the cell with `ev` equal to the count of its window (1) before the ghost's branch, which would put 4 back, can be reached. Run on `rn30` with and without `ghost { min 4, life 60 }`: six occupied readings at 40 m, cleared after 6 free readings both ways, re-marked on the next occupied reading, and **the second clear after 1 free reading both ways**. So this round needs **one new field in the module, `ghost.carry`** (boolean, default false): when set, a cell marked by the ordinary rule while it is a ghost takes `ev = max(count, ghost.min)`; without it the follow-up 18 ghost is unchanged. If it meant the second hold on a second range (the range at the ping and the nearest ever read, the longer taken), follow-up 15's T1 to T3 found that which range the gate goes by does not matter (`re30` 153, `mk30` 153, `mn30` 155) and that is not the reading taken; if it meant the ghost behind a 2-of-3 mark, that is follow-ups 18, 29 and 30. Say so before the build.

**Registered after results were read, and said so.** Follow-ups 25 to 31 were registered before any set was flown. This one was not: the results of follow-ups 7 to 17 and 20 had been read when it was written, and what it asks is shaped by them: follow-up 15's (the ramp, not the range, carries; `rn30` held the bar with `inc` level on count, and the weakest cell was the gaps kind), follow-up 17's (a first-reading mark costs under false returns, and a hold that restores evidence is a second hold on exactly the cells that mark most readily) and follow-up 20's (a hold makes the family of confirmations one rule, so none is wanted here). Follow-up 18's set is flown and its verdict is **unread**; it is the round that tests the ghost behind a confirmation. The sets of follow-ups 19 and 21 to 24 are unread or unflown. The predictions below were written with those results in view, which the registrations of follow-ups 8 to 31 were not.

**The question.** A cell's hold is `min(ev, g(r))`, and clearing a cell zeroes `ev`. A wall seen twenty times, held for twelve free readings and cleared by a dropout of thirty pings is, when the dropout ends and one ping finds it, a cell of one reading; a second dropout, a leak or a run of misses clears it at the first free reading. The carry says the vessel owes a cell it once held a short memory of that. Its price is the ghost's price: a persistent false return that once earned 4 occupied readings comes back held, and the persistent mix is built to find those.

**The rule** (follow-up 15's detector with one switch). Each cell keeps `ev` (occupied readings since it was last cleared, capped at 255, hold at most 30) and a ghost counter. A free cell is **marked** at one occupied reading. A marked cell is **cleared** after `thr = min(ev, g(r))` consecutive free readings, `g(r) = max(1, round(30 x (1 - r / 200 m)))` (the ramp), with `r` the nearest range the cell has been read occupied from (`min`) or the range at the ping (`ping`). **With the carry**: a cell cleared with `ev` of at least 4 becomes a ghost for 60 readings of that cell, and is marked at an occupied reading in that time with `ev = 4`; after 60 readings it is forgotten (its evidence emptied). With no carry it is follow-up 15's detector.

**Conditions** (everything else as follow-ups 3 to 31):
* **`D30`**: follow-up 15's: whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law and none of follow-up 4's white faults. The bar's.
* **`WD30L`**: follow-up 21's: follow-up 4's four white faults with the false rate 0.01 uniform, and dropouts of 30 pings **leaking** at 0.15 (an occupied reading now and then through a dropout).
* **`P`**: follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix (a bias, dropouts of ten pings, persistent false returns, the range law, first surfaces only): where the carry is called.
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix, a mark of one and the evidence hold of cap 30): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `D30` the five detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`rn30`** (ramp on the nearest range: follow-up 15's designated, here the control), **`rp30`** (ramp on the range at the ping), **`rn30c`** (**the designated: `rn30` with the carry**) and **`rp30c`** (`rp30` with the carry) (5); under `WD30L` `inc`, `rn30` and `rn30c` (3); under `P` `inc`, `rn30` and `rn30c` (3); `rn30c` on a sea with no faults (1). Fourteen arms, 2,520 flights. **The set is HELD37 = seeds 3701 to 3760 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `rn30c` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `D30`**: three cells, follow-up 15's bar on a fresh set. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`WD30L`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar. `rn30` held this bar in follow-up 15, so a verdict here is a statement about the carry only through the predictions.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The carry matters only for a cell that is cleared and then read again within 60 readings; with dropouts of 30 pings arriving at a per-ping rate on each obstacle, two on the same obstacle close together are not common, and a leaking dropout (`WD30L`) already holds a cell by its occupied readings. So the expected gain is small. Against it, the persistent mix returns the same false returns again and again, and each of those that has been read four times is restored at 4 (a hold of 4 readings is short, but the return is exactly where the planner reads it as a wall).

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AG).**
* **AG1** the carry changes little under the dropout: under `D30`, `rn30c` is within 8 of `rn30` (either side). (It acts at the second clear of a cell, which one 30-ping dropout does not bring.)
* **AG2** and little under the leak: under `WD30L`, `rn30c` is within 8 of `rn30` (either side). (A leak already holds the cell by its occupied readings.)
* **AG3** the carry has a price under the persistent mix: under `P`, `rn30c` reaches at least 5 fewer than `rn30`. (A persistent false return that earned its evidence is restored held.)
* **AG4** which range the gate goes by still does not matter with the carry: under `D30`, `rp30c` is within 10 of `rn30c` (either side). (Follow-up 15's T1 and T2.)
* **AG5** `rn30c` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `D30`.
* **AG6** the designated beats the map that forgets under the dropout: under `D30`, `rn30c` reaches more than `inc`. (Follow-up 15's T4 asked 10 more and missed at 4 more; this asks only for more.)
* **AG7** under the persistent mix `P`, `rn30c` is within 10 of `inc` (either side). (Follow-up 15's T8: `rn30` 156 and `inc` 157.)
* **AG8** on a sea with no faults `rn30c` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`rn30c` against `rn30` per kind and per gap (the 24 m gaps especially: a restored cell far away is a wall the fix smears), `rn30c` against `inc` under `WD30L`, and the number of carried re-marks (`detStats.ghostMarks`) are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers for the carry, or that it should restore 4 and not the evidence the cell had; that the carry should be keyed on the cell and not on the obstacle it belongs to; that the ramp, 200 m and a cap of 30 are the right hold (follow-ups 13, 15, 17 and 18 are the rest of that table); that the dropouts are like a real sonar's (they are made up, and so is the leak). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs **a new field in the module** (`ghost.carry`, default false: validation, the mark path, `describe`, gate rows and mutants), two registry rows (`rn30c` and `rp30c`; `rn30` and `rp30` exist), the set HELD37 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD37 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 33: THE RANGE-AWARE DETECTOR WITH THE HOLD THAT EARNS ITS LENGTH (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-hold-range", asked while the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, but NOT blind to results: see the paragraph after the next)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 13's name (`detector-range-hold`: the evidence hold gated by range) with `-range` on the end, the move follow-up 17 made on follow-up 16 and follow-up 31 on follow-up 24, and follow-up 13 itself ends by pointing at it: *"a detector with both ends range-aware and evidence-based is a follow-up on this one"*. Follow-up 17 took that pointer with the memory in the mark (the 2-of-3 of follow-up 14); this round takes it with the **plain run** of follow-up 10, which has no memory: **follow-up 10's detector (a run of three to mark beyond 80 m, a first reading within; its hold of thirty within 80 m and one beyond) with its plain lock replaced by follow-up 13's evidence hold** (cap 30 within 80 m, one free reading beyond; a marked cell is cleared after `min(30, occ)` free readings, `occ` its occupied readings since it was last cleared). Follow-up 10 was NOT SUPPORTED for the reason its own note gives: a near false return is marked at once and a plain lock holds it thirty readings, and *"the lock here is a plain count, the evidence hold ... is not"*; this round asks whether the evidence hold is the repair. If it meant the memory in the mark, that is follow-up 17's `rm23re30` and is an arm here; if it meant the clear's fraction depending on range, that is follow-up 31; say so before the build.

**Registered after results were read, and said so.** Follow-ups 25 to 31 were registered before any set was flown. This one was not: the results of follow-ups 7 to 18 and 20 had been read when it was written, and the predictions below were written with them in view: follow-up 10's (`rd` collapses), follow-up 13's, follow-up 17's (`rd` 14 of 180, `re30` 112, `rm23re30` 118, the uniform pair 145 under range-law false returns with dropouts), follow-up 18's (taking the range gate off the hold paid +10) and follow-up 20's (under a hold the confirmations are one rule). The sets of follow-ups 19 and 21 to 24 are unread or unflown. The registrations of follow-ups 8 to 31 were not written with a result in view.

**The question.** The range-aware front end is the idea that a far reading deserves a repeat and a near one does not. Its failure in follow-up 10 was at the back (the plain lock); in follow-ups 17 and 18 the range-aware pair with the evidence hold and a 2-of-3 mark was still below the uniform pair. This round asks whether the **run** (three in a row far, one near) with the evidence hold is any different from the 2-of-3, and whether the hold's range gate is worth keeping behind it.

**The rule** (follow-up 10's detector with the lock replaced). A free cell **farther than `near`** (80 m, from the believed position at the ping, inclusive within) is marked after 3 consecutive occupied readings; one **within** `near` after 1. A marked cell is cleared after `min(clear, occ)` consecutive free readings, `clear` = 30 within `near` and 1 beyond (**gated**: `r3re30`) or 30 at every range (**ungated**: `r3e30`).

**Conditions** (everything else as follows-ups 3 to 32):
* **`R3D30`**: follow-up 17's: follow-up 4's four white faults with the misses and the false returns following the range law (mean false rate 0.03) and whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law. The bar's.
* **`W`**: the same four faults with the false rate 0.01 **uniform**, no dropouts (the law NOT holding).
* **`P`**: follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix.
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the six detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`re30`** (follow-up 13's designated: a mark of one, the gated evidence hold), **`rd`** (follow-up 10's designated: the run and the plain lock), **`rm23re30`** (follow-up 17's designated: the 2-of-3 far and the gated evidence hold), **`r3e30`** (the run with the evidence hold at every range) and **`r3re30`** (**the designated: the run with the gated evidence hold**); `inc`, `re30` and `r3re30` under `W` (3) and under `P` (3); `r3re30` on a sea with no faults (1). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD38 = seeds 3801 to 3860 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `r3re30` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30`**: three cells, follow-up 17's bar on a fresh set. **SUPPORTED** if all three, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if two, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`W`, `P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The evidence hold cures the near false return (it has one occupied reading, so it is cleared at the first free one), which is the cure for follow-up 10's collapse. It does not touch the front end: a first-reading mark within 80 m still takes every near false return and every smear edge as a wall for at least a ping, and a run of three beyond 80 m needs three in a row against a miss rate of 0.2 (three readings of a real wall at a hit rate of 0.8 are a run 51% of the time and a 2-of-3 90% of the time), so a far wall is marked late and a dropout that begins before the third reading hides it from the hold altogether. Follow-up 17's range-aware pair was well below the uniform pair; the bet here is that the run changes that less than the hold does.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AH).**
* **AH1** the evidence hold repairs follow-up 10's detector: under `R3D30`, `r3re30` reaches at least 50 more than `rd`. (Follow-up 17: `rd` reached 14 of 180, with near false returns held thirty readings.)
* **AH2** the run is no different from the memory behind a hold: under `R3D30`, `r3re30` is within 10 of `rm23re30` (either side). (Follow-up 20's Y1: under a hold the confirmations are one rule.)
* **AH3** the gate on the hold costs here too: under `R3D30`, `r3e30` reaches at least 5 more than `r3re30`. (Follow-up 18's J1.)
* **AH4** a far run filters what a mark of one does not: under `R3D30`, `r3re30` reaches at least 5 more than `re30`. (A far false return is read once and never confirmed.)
* **AH5** `r3re30` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AH6** the first-reading mark costs when the law fails: under `W`, `r3re30` reaches at least 10 fewer than `inc`. (Follow-up 17's Q5 and Q6.)
* **AH7** and under the persistent mix: under `P`, `r3re30` reaches at least 5 fewer than `inc`. (Follow-up 18's J7 and follow-up 17's Q7.)
* **AH8** on a sea with no faults `r3re30` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`r3re30` against `rm23re30` and `re30` per kind and per gap, and the collision counts, are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That a run of three, 80 m, a cap of 30 and one-for-one are the right numbers (a run of two is the same detector as follow-up 20's `m22e30` with a first-reading mark within 80 m, and was not flown); that the range law is true of a real sonar; that the plain run and the evidence hold are the only ends worth pairing (a K-of-N clear, a likelihood sum and the filtered fix are untried). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs **no new field in the module**, two registry rows (`r3re30` and `r3e30`; the other arms exist), the set HELD38 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD38 is.

### CORRECTION 1 TO FOLLOW-UP 33 (2026-11-03; written BEFORE any code for it, and found when follow-up 36 was registered and follow-up 19 was re-read; nothing below moves a prediction)

**`r3re30` is not a new detector.** The registration above says the build adds two registry rows (`r3re30` and `r3e30`) and that "the other arms exist". The detector `r3re30` (a run of three beyond 80 m, a first reading within, the gated evidence hold of cap 30) is **exactly follow-up 19's `crre30`**: the registry row is `crre30: spec(RUN(3), EVR(), { markNear: RUN(1) })`, and follow-up 19's table lists that cell ("run of 3 far, 1 near" by "evidence hold") as "flown here, `crre30`", with its prediction B8 (`crre30` within 10 of `rd` under `R3D30`). The registration was written from memory of the rows and did not check for it. **What changes:** only `r3e30` is a new row (one, not two), and `r3re30` is an alias of `crre30`. **What follow-up 33 still adds:** follow-up 19 flies `crre30` under `R3D30` only, against `inc`, `rm23`, `rd`, `rm23lk` and `rm23re30`; this round flies it on a fresh set (HELD38) under `R3D30`, `W`, `P` and a clean sea, with the hold at every range (`r3e30`) and a mark of one (`re30`) beside it, so it is that detector's replication on `R3D30` and its first reading on the other three seas. **Unchanged:** the arms, the designated, the bar, the verdict rule and the predictions, and that follow-up 19's set (unread when this was written) is not shaped into it. If it should be dropped because follow-up 19 answers it, say so before the build.

## FOLLOW-UP 34: THE GHOST ON THE RAMP HOLD, CONFIRMED (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-hold-range-confirm-hold", asked while the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, but NOT blind to results: see the paragraph after the next)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 27's name (`detector-hold-range-confirm`: which range the confirmed hold goes by, whether the gate is a step or a ramp) with `-hold` on the end, the move follow-up 18 made on follow-up 17, follow-up 29 on follow-up 24 and follow-up 32 on follow-up 15. Follow-up 27's detector is kept as it was (the 2-of-3 mark at every range, the confirmed clear of follow-up 21: `K = ceil(0.8 L)` of the last `L = min(g(r), occ)` readings free, `g` the ramp `max(1, round(30 (1 - r / 200 m)))` on the nearest range the cell has been read occupied from) and **a second hold is put on what was held**, in the one way follow-up 18 and follow-up 29 define it: **the ghost** (a cell cleared with at least 4 occupied readings keeps its window and evidence for 60 readings of that cell; read occupied in that time it is marked again at that reading, with its evidence put back to 4; no neighbour test: that is follow-up 23's). The ghost works under a 2-of-3 mark (it did not under a mark of one: follow-up 32), so **no new module field is needed**. The other second hold of follow-ups 18 and 29, taking the range gate off the hold, is an arm here as the control, since the gate is follow-up 27's subject. If it meant something else, say so before the build.

**Registered after results were read, and said so.** Follow-ups 25 to 31 were registered before any set was flown. This one was not: the results of follow-ups 7 to 18 and 20 had been read when it was written, and the predictions below were written with them in view: follow-up 15's (the ramp, not the range, carries), follow-up 18's (the ghost added +9 to the gated hold and +5 to the hold at every range, and its price under the persistent mix was not visible in the totals) and follow-up 20's (under a hold the confirmations are one rule). **The confirmed clear itself (follow-up 21), the confirmed gated hold (follow-up 24) and the memory confirmation with the lock (follow-up 19) are unread or unflown**, so what is predicted here about the confirmed clear is made without their results. The registrations of follow-ups 8 to 31 were not written with a result in view.

**The question.** The ramp holds a far cell for a few readings (12 at 120 m, 6 at 160 m) and a near one for up to 30; a dropout of 30 pings clears the far cells and the confirmed clear (0.8 of the window) clears a leaking dropout sooner still. The ghost puts a cleared cell back at one occupied reading. Whether that second hold pays on the hold that already reaches the 24 m gaps (follow-up 15) and what it costs under the persistent false returns is the question; the ramp's range (`min` or `ping`) is carried along because follow-up 27 asks it of the confirmed hold.

**The rule** (follow-up 27's detector with a switch). Each cell keeps a window of its last 32 readings, `occ` (occupied readings since last cleared) and a ghost counter. A free cell is **marked** when at least 2 of its last 3 readings were occupied, at every range; a marked cell is **cleared** when at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` of its last `L = min(g(r), occ)` readings were free, `r` the nearest range it has been read occupied from (`min`) or the range at the ping (`ping`). **With the ghost**: a cell cleared with `occ` of at least 4 becomes a ghost for 60 readings of that cell, and is marked at an occupied reading in that time with `occ = 4`; after 60 it is forgotten.

**Conditions** (everything else as follows-ups 3 to 33):
* **`R3D30`**: follow-up 17's: follow-up 4's four white faults with the misses and the false returns following the range law (mean false rate 0.03) and whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings with the range law.
* **`WD30L`**: follow-up 21's: the four white faults with the false rate 0.01 uniform, and dropouts of 30 pings **leaking** at 0.15.
* **`P`**: follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix: where the ghost is called.
* And a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the 2-of-3 mark): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `R3D30` the five detectors **`inc`** (the map that forgets), **`m23e30kg`** (follow-up 29's designated: the confirmed hold at every range with the ghost), **`m23rn30k`** (follow-up 27's designated: the ramp on the nearest range, no ghost), **`m23rn30kg`** (**the designated: the same with the ghost**) and **`m23rp30kg`** (the ramp on the range at the ping, with the ghost); under `WD30L` `inc`, `m23e30kg`, `m23rn30k` and `m23rn30kg` (4); under `P` `inc`, `m23rn30k` and `m23rn30kg` (3); and `m23rn30kg` on a sea with no faults (1). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD39 = seeds 3901 to 3960 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m23rn30kg` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `R3D30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**The tension, said before the data.** The ghost restores a far cell the ramp let go, which is exactly what a dropout does to it, and the restored cell is held at 4 (shorter than 12 at 120 m): the second hold is short where the first is short. And the ghost re-marks a persistent false return that once earned 4 readings, which the persistent mix is built to find; follow-up 18's totals did not show that price, at 16 re-marks a flight, so it may be small. The ramp's own price (it gives up near hold for the 80 to 160 m band) is not changed by the ghost.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AI).**
* **AI1** the ghost helps the ramp hold: under `R3D30`, `m23rn30kg` reaches at least 5 more than `m23rn30k`. (Follow-up 18's J3 and J4, +9 and +5.)
* **AI2** and answers the leak: under `WD30L`, `m23rn30kg` reaches at least 5 more than `m23rn30k`. (The confirmed clear clears a leaking dropout sooner; the ghost restores it at one reading.)
* **AI3** the ghost's price under the persistent mix is small: under `P`, `m23rn30kg` is within 8 of `m23rn30k` (either side). (Follow-up 18: `rm23e30g@P` 159 against `inc` 160 at 16 re-marks a flight.)
* **AI4** the ramp needs no hold at every range once there is a ghost: under `R3D30`, `m23rn30kg` is within 8 of `m23e30kg` (either side). (Follow-up 15's ramp reaches what the step does not; follow-up 18's J1.)
* **AI5** which range the ramp goes by still does not matter: under `R3D30`, `m23rp30kg` is within 10 of `m23rn30kg` (either side). (Follow-up 15's T2.)
* **AI6** `m23rn30kg` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `R3D30`.
* **AI7** and under `WD30L`.
* **AI8** on a sea with no faults `m23rn30kg` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
`m23rn30kg` against `m23rn30k` per kind and per gap (the 24 m gaps especially) and the number of ghost re-marks (`detStats.ghostMarks`) are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers for the ghost, that it should be a count of readings and not a length of time, or that the ghost is the only second hold (a decayed evidence, a hold on the neighbours, a K-of-N clear are untried); that 80 m, 200 m, 30, 0.8 and 2-of-3 are the right numbers; that the leak is a measured flicker. Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs **no new field in the module**, two registry rows (`m23rn30kg` and `m23rp30kg`; `m23rn30k` is follow-up 27's row and `m23e30kg` follow-up 29's, added with them), the set HELD39 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD39 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 35: THE GHOST BEHIND THE MARK RULE, WITH THE CLEAR CONFIRMED (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-confirm-hold-confirm-hold", asked while the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, but NOT blind to results: see the paragraph after the next)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 22's name (`detector-confirm-hold-confirm`: which mark rule when the hold is there and its clear is confirmed) with `-hold` on the end, the move follow-ups 18, 29, 30, 32 and 34 made: **a second hold, on what has been confirmed**, which is **the ghost** (a cell cleared with at least 4 occupied readings keeps its window and evidence for 60 readings of that cell; read occupied in that time it is marked again at that reading, with its evidence put back to 4; no neighbour test: that is follow-up 23's). Follow-up 22's detector is kept as it was (the mark rule varied; the evidence hold of cap 30 at every range with the confirmed clear of follow-up 21, `K = ceil(0.8 L)` of the last `L = min(30, occ)` readings free), and its question gets a second half: **the ghost re-marks at one reading whatever the mark rule says, so with a ghost the mark rule matters only at a cell's first marking**, and the rules that are slowest to mark (a run of three, 3 of 4) are the ones the ghost should help most. The name is also the four stages of follow-up 23 (`hold-confirm-hold-confirm`) in another order; follow-up 23 takes each stage out of **one** detector with the **neighbour** test on the ghost, and this round varies the mark rule with the plain ghost. If it meant follow-up 23's detector or something else, say so before the build.

**Registered after results were read, and said so.** Follow-ups 25 to 31 were registered before any set was flown. This one was not: the results of follow-ups 7 to 18 and 20 had been read when it was written, and the predictions below were written with them in view: follow-up 18's (the ghost added +9 to the gated hold and +5 to the hold at every range, its price under the persistent mix was not visible in the totals, the top four detectors were level) and follow-up 20's (under a hold the confirmations are one rule, spread 6 of 180; strictness cost where the misses are many). **The confirmed clear (follow-up 21), the mark rule with it (follow-up 22) and the confirmed gated hold (follow-up 24) are unread or unflown**, so what is predicted here about the confirmed clear is made without their results. The registrations of follow-ups 8 to 31 were not written with a result in view.

**The rule** (follow-up 22's detector with a switch). Each cell keeps a window of its last 32 readings, `occ` (occupied readings since last cleared) and a ghost counter. A free cell is **marked** when at least `M` of its last `N` readings were occupied (`occ` then starts at that count); a marked cell is **cleared** when at least `K = ceil(0.8 x L)` of its last `L = min(30, occ)` readings were free, at every range. **With the ghost**: a cell cleared with `occ` of at least 4 becomes a ghost for 60 readings of that cell, and is marked at an occupied reading in that time with `occ = 4`, whatever the mark rule; after 60 it is forgotten.

**Conditions** (exactly follow-up 22's, everything else as follow-ups 3 to 34): **`WD30`** (follow-up 4's white faults with the false rate 0.01 uniform, with whole-obstacle dropouts of 30 pings and the range law), **`WD30L`** (the same with the dropouts leaking, each reading of a dropped-out obstacle still a normal return with chance 0.15), **`P`** (follow-up 5's `pall`, the persistent mix: where the ghost is called) and a sea with no faults.

**Arms** (every arm but `exact` is the 200 m sonar with the white 3 m fix and the clear of `f80`): `exact`; the clean `long+fix+forget`; under `WD30` the six detectors **`m11f80`** (a mark of one: the control that repairs nothing, and on which a ghost does nothing), **`m23f80`** (follow-up 21's designated) and **`m33f80`** (follow-up 22's designated), without the ghost, and **`m23f80g`**, **`m33f80g`** (**the designated: a run of three with the ghost**) and **`m34f80g`** with it; under `WD30L` `m23f80`, `m33f80`, `m23f80g` and `m33f80g` (4); under `P` `m33f80` and `m33f80g` (2); and `m33f80g` on a sea with no faults (1). Fifteen arms, 2,700 flights. **The set is HELD40 = seeds 4001 to 4060 of each of `hard-islands`, `hard-gaps` and `hard-current`** (180 scenarios), fresh, flown ONCE.

**Bar and verdict.** The designated `m33f80g` reaches **at least 80% of what `exact` reaches in EACH kind under `WD30` and under `WD30L`**: six cells. **SUPPORTED** if all six, **PARTLY SUPPORTED** if at least four, **NOT SUPPORTED** otherwise. (`P` and the clean sea are in the predictions, not the bar.)

**Why `m33f80g` is the designated, and the tension, said before the data.** It is follow-up 22's designated (the rule that separates a wall from the smear best, and the slowest to mark a real cell) with the stage that repairs slowness; if the ghost works as designed it removes the run's price at every re-marking and keeps its separating power at the first marking. Against it: the ghost re-marks a smear edge cell that once earned 4 readings at one reading, which is the thing the run of three and the confirmed clear were together keeping out; and the ghost does nothing for the first marking, where a dropout that begins before the third reading hides an obstacle from the hold altogether.

**Predictions (scored held or missed, moving nothing; pooled over the three kinds unless said; each with its reason; the ids carry the letters AJ).**
* **AJ1** the ghost helps the strict run where cells are cleared: under `WD30L`, `m33f80g` reaches at least 5 more than `m33f80`. (A leaking dropout clears cells and a run of three takes three clean readings to bring them back; follow-up 22's X2 and follow-up 30's AE1.)
* **AJ2** and helps it more than the light rule: under `WD30L`, (`m33f80g` minus `m33f80`) exceeds (`m23f80g` minus `m23f80`) by at least 3. (Follow-up 30's AE2.)
* **AJ3** the ghost's price under the persistent mix is small: under `P`, `m33f80g` is within 8 of `m33f80` (either side). (Follow-up 18: `rm23e30g@P` 159 against `inc` 160.)
* **AJ4** with a ghost the mark rule is one rule: under `WD30`, the three ghost arms (`m23f80g`, `m33f80g`, `m34f80g`) have pooled counts within 10 of each other (largest minus smallest). (The ghost re-marks whatever the rule says; follow-up 20's Y1 for the first marking.)
* **AJ5** every confirmation still repairs the white false returns: under `WD30`, each of the three ghost arms reaches at least 20 more than `m11f80`. (Follow-up 22's X4.)
* **AJ6** `m33f80g` holds the bar (80% of exact) in every kind under `WD30`.
* **AJ7** and under `WD30L`.
* **AJ8** on a sea with no faults `m33f80g` is within 8 of the clean `long+fix+forget`.
The ghost's effect under `WD30` (`m33f80g` against `m33f80`, `m23f80g` against `m23f80`), the per-kind and per-gap counts and the number of ghost re-marks (`detStats.ghostMarks`) are flown and reported and not predicted.

**What it would not say.** That 0.8, 30, 4 occupied readings and a life of 60 are the right numbers; that a run of three is the best of the family or that the family is the only thing a ghost could be asked of (a 4-of-5, a likelihood sum, a neighbour test are untried or follow-up 23's); that the leak is a measured flicker (it is made up, follow-up 21). Not a different pilot, a different generator or the moving ships. A result is a statement about this generator and these models.

**The build waits.** The module and the study engine of follow-ups 8 to 24 are frozen until their sets are flown; this round needs **no new field in the module**, three registry rows (`m23f80g`, `m33f80g` and `m34f80g`; `m23f80g` is the detector follow-up 23 calls `gU`; the other arms are follow-ups 21 and 22's rows), the set HELD40 and a spec, added **after** the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 are flown and **before** HELD40 is.

## FOLLOW-UP 36: WHICH RANGE THE PLAIN LOCK GOES BY (Keith, 2026-11-03: "lets also do nav-sensed-detector-range-confirm-hold-range", asked while the sets of follow-ups 8 to 24 were being flown; REGISTERED BEFORE ANY CODE FOR IT AND BEFORE ITS SET IS FLOWN, but NOT blind to results: see the paragraph after the next)

**What the name was taken to mean, said first.** No backlog entry had that name. It is follow-up 19's name (`detector-range-confirm-hold`: the range-aware 2-of-3 confirmation with the plain range-gated lock, `rm23lk`) with `-range` on the end, the move follow-up 15 made on follow-up 13 and follow-up 27 on follow-up 24: **which range the lock goes by, and whether its gate is a step or a ramp**. Follow-up 19's detector is kept (2-of-3 beyond 80 m, a first reading within; a **plain** lock, `thr = g(r)` free readings in a row with no evidence: it is the lock of follow-up 8, not the evidence hold) and the lock's gate is varied as follow-up 15 varied the evidence hold's: `g` a **step** (30 within 80 m, 1 beyond: `rm23lk`) or a **ramp** `max(1, round(30 (1 - r / 200 m)))`, and `r` the range at the **ping** or the **nearest** the cell has been read occupied from. If it meant the range-aware mark's range (follow-up 14's, already here) or the evidence hold's range (follow-ups 15 and 17), or the confirmed clear's fraction by range (follow-up 31), say so before the build.

**Registered after results were read, and said so.** Follow-ups 25 to 31 were registered before any set was flown.
